策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
延續前兩季優於預期的趨勢，企業 EPS 若能上修有望部分抵銷高利率對本益比的壓縮
效果。換言之，後續市場表現未必只由 Fed 政策主導，企業實際獲利能否持續兌現，
將是支撐科技與半導體股中期走勢的重要力量。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦美股財報與 Fed 決策，科技股中期趨勢料不致有變：近期市場同時面臨財報
前的觀望態勢、美債殖利率偏高及 AI 資本支出疑慮等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能
受到壓抑，後續宜關注 10 月美股財報對企業獲利與資本支出展望，以及 FOMC 會議
所釋出的政策訊號。中期而言，目前AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡

象，科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，但在利率維持高檔及市場評價偏高的環境下，後
續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出的實際變化。
2. 中天期債券風險報酬仍較均衡 ：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使短端殖利率持續受到 Fed 政策路
徑影響，而 20～30 年超長端仍面臨財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價偏高等結構性壓
力。相較之下，目前殖利率已位於歷史相對高檔，若後續核心通膨或能源價格改善，
中段殖利率具有回落條件，同時其存續期間風險又低於超長端，因此現階段以中天期
美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

上週重點訊息
◼ Fed 維持利率不變，兩位委員主張升息：美國聯準會於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 的表決結果，
將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持在 3.75%至 4%，其中理事庫克與克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪
克對該決議投下反對票，並主張應升息 1 碼。Fed 在聲明中表示，經濟活動正以強勁步調擴
張，但通膨仍然偏高。在已有兩位委員主張升息的情況下，後續通膨數據將是 Fed 12 月是
否升息的重要影響因素。
◼ Alphabet 自由現金流轉負幅度超出預期：Alphabet 於 10 月 28 日公布第三季財報，自由現

金流為負 90.7 億美元，明顯低於分析師預期的負 27 億美元，同時 Alphabet 維持全年
1,950 億至 2,050 億美元的資本支出指引，顯示 AI 基礎建設投資仍持續消耗公司現金。
Alphabet 該季自由現金流明顯轉負，也引發市場對於科技巨頭資本支出的疑慮，並帶動整體
科技類股出現修正。
◼ 英特爾因 PC 需求疲弱下修第四季財測：英特爾於 10 月 29 日公布第三季財報，營收年增
21%至 165.1 億美元，高於公司原先財測；其中資料中心與 AI 部門營收年增 78%，為 15 年
來最強勁的成長，但以 PC 為主的客戶端運算部門營收則年減 5%。公司指出，由於記憶體短
缺推升 PC 售價，加上企業 IT 預算受限，使 PC 需求轉弱。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：本次財報季顯示雲端營收加速成
長與未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求仍強；但部分企業的資本支出攀升、自由現金流轉弱，加
上 Fed 可能於 12 月升息，短線科技股波動恐將延續。然而中期而言，AI 產業將由全面成長

轉向基本面分化，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健且 AI 投資已能轉化為獲利的企業，風險報酬相
對有利；對高度依賴外部融資、自由現金流持續為負的企業則宜維持審慎。
◼ 5 至 10 年期美債及投資等級公司債兼顧票息收益與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府
與企業發債供給維持高檔，可能使市場利率中期仍處於較高水準，但近期公債殖利率回落，
顯示市場避險需求升溫，支撐債券買盤；整體而言，短天期債券面臨未來利率下降後的再投
資風險，長天期債券則較容易受到公債供給增加、期限溢酬及利率波動影響，5-10 年期美國
公債與投資級債券則較能兼顧息收與利率風險。

一、 美國—PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和
訊號 
標普全球 (S&P Global) 公布， 2026 年 9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數 (PMI 
Composite Output Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4， 連續第四個月攀升 ， 創2021
年 7 月以來新高；其中服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)由 56.5 升至 58.7，創
59 個月高點，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 由 53.9 升至 57.0，創
2022 年 5 月以來最高水準，顯示美國企業活動持續強勁擴張。受國內市場需求穩
健帶動，製造業與服務業新訂單同步加速成長，推升整體產出增速升至逾 5 年來
最快水準，企業亦積極擴增人力，就業成長速度創 2022 年 6 月以來新高。另一
方面，積壓訂單增加及供應鏈延遲加劇，反映產能利用率持續攀升；加以近期油
價上漲推升燃料及運輸成本，使企業投入成本增幅升至 2022 年 10 月以來最高，
帶動售價漲幅回升，顯示通膨壓力有所升溫。儘管如此，企業對未來一年營運前
景仍維持正向看法，反映市場對美國經濟韌性及需求持續擴張仍具信心。 
2026 年 8 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 68.4 萬戶 ， 較上月增加6.4%，
但較去年同期下降 2.0%，顯示新屋銷售較前月回升，惟仍低於去年同期水準。地
區別表現分歧，其中中西部及南部分別較上月增加 84.9%及 6.9%，東北部及西部
則分別減少 36.1%及 15.2%。價格方面，8 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.37 萬美元，較
上月小升 0.4%，但較去年同期下降 5.8%。供給方面，8 月底經季節性調整後待售
新屋數量為 48.3 萬戶，與上月持平，較去年同期減少 2.0%；按目前銷售速度估
算，供給月數為 8.5 個月，低於上月的 9.0 個月，與去年同期大致持平。整體而
言，8 月美國新屋銷售較前月回升，主要受中西部與南部銷售增加帶動，惟仍低
於去年同期，顯示需求動能尚未完全恢復。價格方面，中位價較去年同期下滑，
反映市場價格仍有調整壓力；同時待售新屋維持在 48 萬戶以上，供給月數仍達
8.5 個月，顯示整體供給相對充裕，後續仍須觀察銷售回升是否具持續性。

的 51.7， 月減7.0%、 年減12.7%， 為近四個月低點 ， 並較2026 年 1 月下降約 15%，
顯示消費者信心持續轉弱。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由51.9 降至 50.9，月減

1.9%、 年減15.7%； 消費者預期指數則由51.5 降至 46.3， 月減10.1%、 年減10.4%，

年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，9 月美國消費信心再度降溫，顯示高物價、燃
料價格與貿易不確定性持續壓抑民眾信心，後續仍須觀察通膨預期升溫是否進一
步影響消費動能。 
中國國家主席習近平於 2026 年 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪問美國，並於 9 月 24 日
在華府白宮與美國總統川普舉行峰會。會後，美中雙方未發布聯合聲明，而是分
別公布成果清單，顯示雙方在穩定雙邊關係及維持高層溝通方面取得部分共識，
包括同意建構「基於尊重、公平、對等的建設性戰略穩定關係」、推動 300 億美
元非敏感商品對等降稅安排、建立人工智慧 (AI)或「超級智慧」對話與風險溝通
機制，以及持續就稀土與關鍵礦產供應鏈議題進行磋商。此外，美方並表示中國
將於 2027 年及 2028 年分別進口至少 1,000 萬公噸美國煤炭。值得注意的是，台
灣議題並未出現在雙方公布的成果文件中；另一方面，中方則額外強調兩軍危機
溝通機制及合作搜尋失蹤美軍遺骸等事項。整體而言，本次川習會有助延續美中
對話與降低短期緊張情勢，惟雙方在關稅、科技競爭、供應鏈安全及其他戰略議
題上的結構性分歧仍未完全化解，後續談判進展及雙邊互動方向仍有待持續觀察。 
二、歐元區—9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，且為連續四個月上
升。其中服務業PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，創近 10 個月高點，主要反映 AI 與國防投資
活動增加，帶動商業服務需求，抵銷能源價格上漲對消費購買力的負面影響。製
造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7，不過，德國萊茵河水位下降，沿岸較為發達的化工、製
藥業物流成本增加，因而暫時壓抑製造業上行空間。值得注意的是，由於國際原
油價格回升，綜合投入、產出物價指數皆上升至近 4 個月最高。故儘管 9 月經濟
表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲央行的鷹派態度，使得

### Page 7

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

6

10 月升息一碼的可能性些微增加，亦強化未來升息預期，一般認為最終政策利率
(目前為 2.5%)將會落在 3%-3.25%。 
三、日本—9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性 
 日本 9 月綜合 PMI 由 53.5 降至 52.5，製造業與服務業同步回落，經濟成長動
能較前兩個月的高檔有所降溫。製造業 PMI 下跌 0.8 點至 54.1，雖然產出、新訂
單及出口訂單增速放緩，但外部需求仍相當強勁，尤其 AI 相關需求可能持續帶
動出口表現，加上企業開始重建庫存、供應鏈瓶頸尚未完全解除，預料補庫存需
求將在未來數月繼續支撐生產成長。服務業 PMI 下降 0.9 點至 51.6，受到工資、
運輸及能源成本上升影響 ， 需求動能略有降溫 ， 但積壓訂單與出口業務有所改善 。
另，投入成本與售價漲幅均較前期放緩，顯示通膨壓力正逐步緩解，但整體物價
水準仍維持高檔。此外，企業對未來景氣的信心持續改善，未來活動指數升至七
個月高點，反映企業普遍預期經濟擴張趨勢仍可望延續。 
四、中國—美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-7 月)略增 0.1 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 1.9 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 31.1%、71.8%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增20.1%、109.9%)、化學原料
及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.1%、51%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.5%、2.9%，但獲利分別年減 5.2%、16%。非金屬礦物製品業營
收、獲利下滑 9.5、46.7%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減 1.1%、62.4%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，
以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或
政府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
展望未來，儘管美中雙方互相降低等值 300 億美元產品關稅，惟考量雙方每
年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程設備、電動車、
軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，因此實質效益有限。另外，雖然美中雙方同意延長貿
易休戰，但休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給予中國稀土
供應履約的壓力。後續將關注美中能否在休戰期間就開放市場、科技競爭、供應

7

鏈安全等議題取得進一步共識，若談判不如預期，美國仍可能重新祭出新的反制
措施 ， 而中國亦可能運用稀土作為談判籌碼 ， 經貿關係不確定性仍是較高 。 此外 ，
預計 10 月 8 日舉行歐中貿易會談，若談判未能取得突破，歐盟對中國的貿易限
制恐進一步升級，對中國出口形成額外壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
亞 洲 開 發 銀 行 (ADB) 最 新 經 濟 展 望 (Asian Development Outlook, ADO 
September 2026 )上修 2026 年亞洲及太平洋地區經濟體 2026 年經濟成長率(GDP)
至 4.7%(7 月預估 4.5%)、2027 年為 4.5%。ADB 認為，亞洲開發中經濟體雖面臨
中東衝突延長、俄烏戰爭再升溫、能源價格高檔震盪及強烈聖嬰現象等逆風，但
在政府刺激政策、民間投資及 AI 帶動科技出口支撐下，區域經濟仍展現韌性。
通膨方面，2026 年通膨率下修至 3.9%，惟能源與糧食價格壓力仍高，2027 年通
膨預測上修至 3.3%。 
區域經濟表現方面 ， 南亞成長最強勁 ，2026 年 GDP 成長率預估上修至 6.4%，
主要受印度內需與投資帶動；東亞則受中國內需疲弱影響，成長維持平穩；東協
國家(ASEAN)上調至 4.7%， 其中越南 、 馬來西亞及泰國受惠於科技與製造業投資
而獲上修。中亞及西亞、太平洋地區則面臨能源及食品價格上漲壓力，成長預測
略為下修至 3.7%、3.0%。(詳下表) 
ADB 認為未來最大風險包括地緣政治衝突升級、聖嬰 現象造成糧食供應衝
擊，以及 AI 相關資產可能出現價格重估。整體而言，AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞
洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨
與信用風險的重要變數。

五、台灣—AI 浪潮推升接單動能，外銷訂單再創新高 
8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，首度突破千億美元大關，其中受惠於 ASIC
晶片需求帶動，資通產品接單翻倍、電子產品接單亦大增 83.9%，惟光學器材因
面板需求維持低檔，年減 5%。非科技產品方面，基本金屬年增 26.8%，係因銅箔
及銅箔基板受惠 AI 相關供應鏈需求及銅價續漲，加以鍍鋅鋼需求回溫；機械年
增 28.3%，係因半導體業者增加資本支出，帶動半導體設備、自動化設備需求延
續；化學品年增 15.4%，主因藥品接單成長，加上產業用化學品需求持續增加；
塑橡膠製品年增 6.7%， 主係塑膠原料受原料上漲而推升產品售價 ， 加上部分塑膠
製品訂單回溫。展望未來，經濟部統計處預估 2026 年外銷訂單有望突破 1 兆美
元，在 AI 相關需求持續擴張帶動下，科技產品表現可望維持強勁，而傳統產業
中下游產品亦可望受惠於相關供應鏈需求，惟上游原物料價格競爭激烈，後續仍
須關注第四季消費旺季是否有相關備貨需求。

STRESS TEST 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

목표주가 37만원, ‘28년 기준으로는 56만원까지 열림 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 37만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27 년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3 배를 적용했다. 이는 ‘00년 이후 25년간 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 
해당한다[표1]. ‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 56%, 5 9%, 4 8%로 지난 25년 평균인 
22%를 크게 상회할 전망이며, 고원형 ROE가 장기간 유지될 것으로 판단한다.  
이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해질 경우 시장의 시야는 ‘28년으로 향할 것이다. 
이 경우 ‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 56만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 43% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -41%를 넘어섰다. 특히 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
이러한 우려가 지속되는 한, 이익의 눈높이가 높아질수록 멀티플은 저렴해질 
뿐이며, DRAM 가격이 예상보다 더 올라갈 것이라는 소식이 주가의 촉매로 
작용하지 못한다. 결국 주가 반등을 위해서는 시장이 상정하고 있는 다운사이클이 
우려만큼 깊지 않을 것이라는 믿음이 필요한 것으로 본다.

STRESS TEST와 HBM 재평가 
① 당사는 고객들이 어느 정도 가격대에서 LTA를 해지하려는 유인이 생길지 
추정해봤다. Micron의 코멘트를 차용하여 ‘22~’23년 수준의 스트레스 테스트를 
시나리오 분석한 결과, 4Q27 고점 대비 DRAM 가격이 60% 수준 빠져야 해지가 
검토될 수 있다[그림2]. 그마저도 다음 공급 부족기의 물량 배정 우선순위 상실 
등 정성적 요인까지 감안하면 실제 해지 가능성은 더욱 낮은 것으로 판단한다. 
② HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 
중장기적으로 확대될 cHBM은 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 
재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 물량과 가격이 결정된다. 수요가 둔화되더라도 범용 
메모리처럼 재고 누적과 가격 급락으로 이어지기보다는 발주 조정이나 계약 
재협상을 통해 영향을 받는 구조다.  
동사 HBM 매출은 ‘26년 28조원에서 ‘28년 215조원으로 증가할 전망이다. 
‘27년 HBM 가격 방향성의 윤곽이 잡힌 현 시점에서, 동사 HBM 매출의 변수는 
가격이 아니라 물량이며, 물량은 고객과 사전에 협의되는 구조다. 과거 메모리 
업황에서는 2년 뒤의 이익을 가늠하기 어려웠으나, 이제는 HBM을 통해 중장기 
실적을 논의할 수 있게 됐다는 점, 그 자체에 주목한다. 
만약 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E (21배, 15배, 12배)를 
적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 19%, 69%, 108%를 설명한다[표3]. 
특히 cHBM이 본격화할 ‘28년에는 HBM만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명될 수 
있다는 의미다. 시장이 통상 받아들이는 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 다르지 
않다.

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간일 전망이다.  
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 업황 지속성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 이 불확실성이 
멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 
구간이 이어질 전망이다. 기회비용은 결국 환원수익률로 보상받아야 하므로, ‘27년 1월 신규 
주주환원 정책이 중요한 변곡점이 될 것으로 판단한다.  
당사는 동사가 ‘27~’29년 FCF의 50%를 주주에게 환원하고, 이 중 70%를 배당, 30%를 
자사주 매입/소각으로 쓰는 것으로 가정하여, 현재 주가 기준 ‘27년 15% 수준의 
주주환원수익률을 기대한다. 미국 10년물 국채 금리가 5% 안팎인 환경에서 무위험 금리의 
3배에 달하는 환원수익률, 그중 배당만으로 10% 수준의 수익률은 그 자체로 희소한 투자 
포인트로 볼 수 있다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는, 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 이후 신규 팹 물량이 
소량 출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 LTA가 이익을 지킨다는 사실이 
숫자로 확인되고 cHBM의 가치가 재평가된다면 순환 할인이 축소될 것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

Document 229: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기 환율 부진에도 영업이익 100조는 넘긴다 
삼성전자 26년 3분기 실적은 이전 전망치를 하회할 전망이다. 매출액은 195.2조원으
로 이전 분기 대비 13.8% 증가하고, 영업이익은 101.7조원으로 13.6% 증가할 것으
로 예상한다. 영업이익률은 52.1%로 전 분기 대비 0.1%p 하락할 전망이다. 원/달러 
환율 영향이 주요 변수로 작용한 것으로 추정한다. 2분기에 집행되지 않았던 성과급 
일부가 3분기에 반영된 영향도 있을 것으로 예상한다. DS 부문은 매출액 149.9조원, 
영업이익은 101.8조원으로 추정하고, 디스플레이 매출액 8.8조원, 영업이익 0.9조
원, MX/네트워크는 매출액 34.3조원, 영업적자 1.1조원으로 예상한다. VD/가전도 
영업적자 규모가 커질 것으로 예상한다.  
주주환원은 주가 상승을 견인할 것 
8월 21일 이후 주주환원에 대한 관심이 커졌다. 자사주 규모는 크지 않겠지만 배당
을 통한 주주환원 규모는 사상 최대 규모일 것으로 예상한다. 글로벌 대표 IT업체와 
비교해도 손색없는 수준이다. 메모리 업체의 주주환원 규모가 100조원 넘게 집행된 
적이 없어서 주가에 어떠한 영향을 미칠 지가 관전 포인트인데 긍정적 역할을 할 것
으로 기대한다. 자사주를 통한 EPS 개선은 아니지만, 안정적이고 지속적인 규모의 
주주환원은 주가를 한층 더 높은 수준에서 평가 받는 계기가 될 것으로 판단한다.  
현저한 저평가 구간, 이전 전망 유지 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 메모리 공급 부족 상황이 장기화될 
것으로 전망하고, 이로 인해 수익성은 현재 수준에서 유지되고, 이익 규모는 안정적
으로 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 현저히 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 목
표주가는 460,000원을 유지한다.  
목표주가 (유지) 460,000원  
현재가 (10/1) 276,000원

### Page 4

삼성전자 (005930)

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
반도체 Check Point 
1. 차별화된 주주환원 전략. 배경은 안정적 이익 규모 
2026년에는 대대적인 규모의 주주환원이 예상된다. 100 조원 내외 수준으로 
예상하는데 상당 부분은 배당으로 집행될 것으로 추정한다. 배당성향이 25%가 유지될 
것으로 예상하기 때문이다. 자사주 매입 여력은 20조원 내외 수준일 것으로 예상한다. 
주주환원은 주가 밸류 상승에 기여할 것으로 기대한다. 일회성이 아니고 장기적으로 
높은 수준의 주주환원이 가능하다는 것은 싸이클 변동에 따른 이익의 변동성이 
낮다는 의미이기도 하다. 
2. AI 모델 개발 속도 조절. 그래도 메모리 수요를 줄일 수는 없다 
Anthropic CEO는 현재 AI 개발 속도가 지나치게 빨라서 인간의 능력을 뛰어 넘는 
임계점에 이를 가능성이 높아져서 개발 속도를 늦출 필요가 있다고 언급했다. 이를 
Meta, Google, SpaceX 수장들이 공감했다. 우선 이들의 공감으로 AI 모델의 개발 
속도가 둔화될 것으로 보고 있지 않다. 또한, 이를 가정하더라도 향후 AI 모델은 
Agent AI로 진화할 것으로 보고 있다. 이에 따른 메모리에 대한 필요성은 급격히 
증가할 것으로 기대한다. 
3. 자신감 넘치는 HBM  
HBM4가 2026년 하반기부터 주력 제품이 될 것으로 전망한다. 26 년 3분기에는 
비중이 40% 내외, 4 분기에는 60%를 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 연초 기대 대비로는 
낮은 수준이다. 삼성전자의 점유율만 보면 연초 대비 높은 수준의 점유율이 예상된다.  
HBM4 물량이 예상보다 늦게 본격화되면서 2026년 HBM 시장 규모는 이전 전망 
대비 축소될 전망이다. HBM4도 늦은 출시로 연내 생산 물량은 이전 전망치인 130억 
Gb을 하회할 것으로 예상한다.  
2027년 HBM4 가격이 최근에 확정이 되었고, 이를 통해서 DRAM 수준의 수익성 
확보가 가능할 것으로 기대한다. 수익성 발목을 잡았던 HBM은 2027년 물량부터는 
영업이익 개선의 변수가 될 것으로 전망한다.

장기적 주주환원은 
안정적 이익이 뒷받침 
Agent AI는 메모리 
수요를 증가시킨다 
HBM4 안정적 진입한 
것으로 판단

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

Document 231: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

작성자(한동희)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다. 
투자판단 3 단계(6 개월기준) 15%이상 → 매수 / -15%~15% → 중립 / -15%미만 → 매도 
SK 증권 유니버스 투자등급 비율 (2026년 10 월 06 일 기준)  매수 96.35% 중립 3.65% 매도 0.00%

3Q26 Review: 악재의 중첩, 
4분기까지 지켜보자 
최정환 CFA

jeonghwan.choi@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 1,640,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
739,000 
방위산업

◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
◼ 컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
◼ 4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
4Q26E-2Q27E 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 25.0x 
적용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 3Q26 실적 아쉬우나 투자포
인트에는 변함 없어 멀티플은 변경 없이 적용. 27년 실적 추정치 상향에 따라 본
업 가치 상승, 26년 스페인 자주포 양산 및 미국 자주포 시제 사업자로 선정되며 
현지화 전략이 유효했음을 증명. 3Q26 실적에 대한 우려를 소화하며 글로벌 피어
들과의 멀티플 격차 축소를 전망함

컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 6.1조원(-6.0% YoY), 영업이익 4,520억원(-46.2% YoY, 
OPM 7.4%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.4조원 및 영업이익 1.1조원을 크게 하회
하는 실적 시현. 하회의 주요 요인으로는 3Q26 필리조선소 적자폭 확대, 지상
방산 부문 실적이 부진한 영향. 지상방산 부문의 경우 매출액 1.2조원(-41.0% 
YoY), 영업이익 220억원(-96.2% YoY, OPM 1.7%)을 시현했는데, 폴란드 관련 
매출이 부재했던 1Q26 영업이익률 16.9%보다도 낮은 실적을 보임. 이는 국내
외 사업 모두에서 악재가 겹쳐 발생했던 것으로 보이는데 국내 사업에서는 마
진율이 낮은 사업이 매출로 인식되었고 수출 사업에서는 폴란드 매출 부재 뿐 
아니라 이집트, 호주 K9 자주포 매출 인식 또한 예상 대비 부진해 고정비 레버
리지 효과로 3Q26 영업이익률이 크게 훼손된 것으로 추정. 이외 대전사업장 복
구에 따른 비용, 기타 사업 충당금 등 총 500억원 수준의 일회성 비용이 발생했
으며 영업외손익에서는 원화 강세에 따른 환손실이 주로 반영되었음

4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향 
1Q26에서 3Q26까지 이뤄지지 않았던 폴란드 EC2 K9 자주포 30문 인도는 
4Q26에 집중될 것으로 보이며 폴란드 EC1 천무 또한 기존 가이던스였던 26년 
천무 40대에서 60대로 인도 대수 상향이 이뤄짐. 이에 따라 4Q26 지상방산 영
업이익률은 22.8%로 회복을 전망. 27년에는 폴란드 K9 자주포 및 천무 인도 대
수 증가로 26년 수출 비중 58.0%에서 77.2%로 증가, 이에 따라 영업이익률 또
한 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 19.8%에서 28.0%로 개선을 전망

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE

vember 2, 2026

IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015, 1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries, 2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks. 4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index. 5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

4 Institute of International Finance, as of September 23, 2026.
5 FactSet, as of October 27, 2026.

4 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

with demand, supply bottlenecks may

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic

from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

Document 315: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?
Date:    1 hour ago
Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Could Midterms Give Wall Street
A Cold Shower?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 2 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. When Gridlock Isn't Good for Stocks
The US midterm elections will be over tomorrow night. According to
betting markets, Democrats have roughly a 95% probability of winning the
House and a 76% probability of taking the Senate (charts).
We suspect that stock investors have yet to fully discount the possibility of
a Democratic sweep of Congress. Some may remain skeptical that polls
confirming strong support for Democrats are accurate. They might reckon

### Page 2

that Democrats are more likely than Republicans to talk to pollsters over
the phone.
In any event, Wall Street traditionally welcomes political gridlock because
it reduces the likelihood that Congress will pass extremist legislation. But
this time could be different for two reasons.
First, deepening partisanship could turn gridlock into damaging political
paralysis at a time when the Bond Vigilantes are already uneasy about
Washington's unsustainable fiscal trajectory. A Democratic sweep could

complicate negotiations over government spending and the debt ceiling.
Prolonged shutdowns and fiscal standoffs could push Treasury yields
higher, weighing on equity valuations.

### Page 3

Second, gridlock's traditional ability to constrain policymaking may not
hold this time. President Donald Trump has relied heavily on executive
authority, with some of his most market-disruptive policies, including tariffs
and military action against Iran, pursued largely without congressional
approval. A Democratic-controlled Congress may therefore do little to
restrain him. Moreover, with the midterms behind him, Trump may feel
even less constrained politically and more willing to pursue controversial

policies through executive action. Hence, a blue wave could deliver a cold
shower for stocks, though we would view a significant selloff as a buying
opportunity given the remarkable strength of the economy and corporate
earnings.
One encouraging development ahead of the elections was today's reports
that the US and Iran are working on a peaceful diplomatic solution to their
war. Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian said he has granted his
government greater authority to pursue peace talks, while President

Trump and Vice President Vance also signaled progress. Hopes for a deal
sent the price of Brent crude oil down 7.6% to $104.77 per barrel (chart).
However, we have been here before with apparent diplomatic
breakthroughs. We still see a high risk of military re-escalation. The bid-
ask spread remains wide, with Tehran demanding sanctions relief, the
release of frozen assets, and an end to the US naval blockade of Iranian
ports, while Washington insists on nuclear concessions. Meanwhile, Iran's

leverage over the Strait of Hormuz has been weakening. According to
shipping data, for roughly half of September, Gulf oil exporters shipped
more crude than they were shipping before the war.

### Page 4

II. US Economy Continues To Roar
The US economy remains strong despite the volatility of the domestic
political and geopolitical scenes. Consider the following:
(1) Construction spending. Construction spending fell 1.9% m/m in
September to $2.19 trillion (saar), reversing most of August's upwardly
revised 2.0% increase. Nevertheless, spending remains just below last
year's record high (chart).
September's decline was driven by a 5.5% m/m drop in private residential

construction, attributable to a 13.0% plunge in home improvements
(chart). Elevated materials costs and higher mortgage rates continue to
weigh on the housing sector. Meanwhile, private nonresidential
construction rose 1.1% m/m, supported by continued investment in data
centers and power infrastructure.

### Page 5

The AI investment boom remains a powerful tailwind. Data center
construction spending surged 8.6% m/m and 90% y/y in September,
reaching a record $96.5 billion annual rate (chart).
(2) Manufacturing. The ISM M-PMI rose from 54.5 in September to 55.5
in October, marking its tenth consecutive month of expansion, the longest
streak since 2022. Production jumped to the highest reading since
November 2021, while new orders edged up to 55.5. Employment also

remained in expansionary territory (chart).

### Page 6

Inflationary pressures remain troublesome. The prices-paid index eased
from 77.9 to 75.2 in October but remained above 70 for the ninth
consecutive month. Regional Fed surveys also point to persistent price
pressures. Higher energy costs, tariffs, and elevated metals prices
continue to boost input costs (chart).
(3) Bank lending. The Fed's latest Senior Loan Officer Opinion Survey
(SLOOS) painted a mixed picture of credit demand. Banks reported
weaker demand for commercial and industrial (C&I) loans, mortgages,

and auto loans in Q4, while credit-card demand strengthened (chart).

### Page 7

Lending standards remained broadly unchanged for C&I loans but
tightened somewhat for commercial real estate loans (chart).
Encouragingly, actual bank lending tells a happier story. C&I loans surged
11.3% y/y through October 21, while total loans and leases increased
7.9% y/y (chart).

### Page 8

Credit growth is broad across bank sizes, with lending up 8.1% y/y at
large banks and 5.4% at small banks (chart).
Meanwhile, banks' allowances for loan and lease losses have remained
relatively stable at around $202 billion, despite the acceleration in lending
(chart).

### Page 9

clause.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

Document 332: European bank M&A is back with a bang

Source PDF: all report pdf/European bank M&A is back with a bang.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

European bank M&A has seen a resurgence in recent years, and
2027 should stay busy. Domestic deals still dominate, while
cross-border consolidation is harder but is also picking up.
Regulation is becoming more supportive, but politics and
execution still matter. Expect more deals, but a transformation
of Europe's banking sector is still some way oﬀ
Jesse Norcross
UniCredit and Commerzbank are at the centre of Europe’s closely watched
push towards cross-border banking consolidation

European bank M&A is back
European bank M&A has seen a strong resurgence, with several
large-scale deals making headlines. UniCredit’s high-stakes
takeover of Commerzbank, once deemed very unlikely but now
moving closer to completion, could be Europe’s most signiﬁcant
cross-border transaction in years. Italy remains one of Europe’s
bank M&A hotbeds, with multiple banks taking part in the ongoing
consolidation wave. Other banks have also been active, and we

expect M&A to remain elevated in 2027.

### Page 2

Year-to-date M&A deal value has already surpassed the 2025 total,
reaching €53bn including the UniCredit-Commerzbank transaction
and based on deals valued at €100m or more. Furthermore, deals
pending or intended total €39bn, which includes the potential
takeover of Banca Monte dei Paschi by Intesa Sanpaolo, currently
valued at around €34bn. All in, this would bring deal value back to
levels not seen since the early 2010s.
European bank M&A volumes have seen a

resurgence

The rationale for European bank M&A has changed. After the
ﬁnancial crisis, deals were mostly about repair, followed by a wave
of deals involving stronger domestic banks buying weaker rivals.
Today, the backdrop has shifted – proﬁtability and capital levels are
stronger, making the obvious cost-eﬃciency story less compelling

on its own. Instead, banks are using M&A to add scale, deposits and
fee-generating diversiﬁcation, while the need to close the gap with
US rivals remains a key driver.
The industrial logic has also strengthened, as ﬁxed costs are rising
in areas such as technology, AI, compliance, as well as personnel.
Rising investment requirements mean that remaining subscale is
becoming more expensive, even for highly proﬁtable banks.

### Page 3

Domestic dominates, but cross-border
competes
UniCredit-Commerzbank remains the sector’s most closely
watched deal and could become the ﬁrst major cross-border
acquisition since the ﬁnancial crisis. UniCredit has slowly been
building its stake rather than a clean takeover and is now deemed
to hold eﬀective control, despite currently owning around 50% of
the shares. Furthermore, it is also not entirely a leap into an
unfamiliar market, as UniCredit already has a signiﬁcant banking

business in Germany through its subsidiary, HVB.
The deal has also faced considerable pushback as well as political
and stakeholder opposition, but is now looking increasingly likely to
proceed. It has highlighted the diﬃculty of pulling oﬀ a large-scale
cross-border deal for European banks. Yet it is now becoming the
clearest live test of whether Europe is genuinely ready for cross-
border bank consolidation.
Domestic deals remain the rule, but we have seen other examples

of cross-border deals. These include BPCE’s acquisition of
Portuguese peer Novo Banco; Erste Bank's acquisition of Santander
Bank Polska; and Crédit Agricole acquiring IKB to increase its
presence in Germany. Some banks are also looking across the
pond, such as Santander, which acquired Webster Financial
Corporation in the US for USD12.5bn.
These transactions are important, but they do not yet amount to a
broad-based cross-border consolidation wave. Many involve banks

already familiar with the target market or assets that complement
an existing local business.
Selected transaction details

### Page 4

Source: Company reports, ING
Italy is still riding the wave
Italy has become one of Europe’s busiest M&A markets, riding a
new wave of consolidation, with Banca Monte dei Paschi (MPS) at
the epicentre. The bank completed the acquisition of Mediobanca in
2025, and this year approached Banco BPM and Banca Generali for
a merger as a counteroﬀensive to Intesa Sanpaolo’s hostile bid for
MPS.
The counteroﬀensive now appears less likely, as key shareholders

have opposed parts of MPS’s merger plans and have also backed
Intesa’s bid. The ECB has also provided authorisation to take control
of MPS, representing an important step for the €34bn takeover.
Alongside this, we had the BPER–Popolare di Sondrio merger earlier
in the year, adding to Italy’s consolidation.
Why is Italy so active in particular? Well, Italy remains quite
fragmented (see below) and still oﬀers a large domestic synergy
pool, even after the wave of M&A that has already taken place.

Furthermore, the country has meaningful industrial scale and oﬀers
further revenue diversiﬁcation opportunities for banks and insurers.
Although the obvious combinations are becoming less evident in
Italy, we expect this to continue to drive M&A activity in 2027.

country (2025)

It's not only about getting bigger
The deal mix is also broadening, as banks are increasingly focusing
on fee income as well as scale, while also stepping into capital-light
businesses. Deals in sectors such as wealth management, asset
management, payments & FX and insurance are likely to feature
prominently.
NatWest Group’s acquisition of Evelyn Partners is a good example.

The combination created the UK’s largest Private Banking and
Wealth Management business, while lifting Natwest’s fee income
by 20%. Similarly, BNP Paribas’s acquisition of AXA Investment
Managers in 2025 created a €1.5tn asset management business,
as the bank is seeking to compete with US asset management
rivals and grow its fee income. In Germany, Deutsche Bank and
DWS were reported to be exploring a potential minority investment
in the life insurance business Frankfurter Leben. This would mark

Deutsche’s return to life insurance after more than a decade.
Consolidation can support bank
performance
But does consolidation actually deliver better outcomes? The
evidence is not entirely clear-cut, but overall points in the right
direction. While increasing scale is not always the best use of
capital, in general, more consolidated markets tend to have more
stable and, in some cases, higher proﬁtability. The ECB is in favour
of further consolidation where appropriate, as it sees the beneﬁts of

### Page 6

diversiﬁcation and eﬃciency. On the other hand, capital can be
reduced following a merger. This could be a downside, as better
capitalised banks tend to enjoy lower funding costs and thus have
more room to lend to the real economy.
The outcome therefore depends on the trade-oﬀ between longer-
term synergies and the upfront capital, execution and integration
costs.
ECB data show an association between more concentrated banking
markets and proﬁtability. As the chart below shows, countries with

a high level of concentration (deﬁned by a higher Herﬁndahl-
Hirschman Index coeﬃcient) tend to have higher net interest
margins on average. Still, net interest margins in Europe are
structurally lower than in the US, where they tend to be between 3–
4%.

Similarly, a higher level of market concentration in European
countries is associated with a lower cost of risk. The correlation is
modest, with some clear outliers, but the numbers show that
greater concentration can lead to lower asset quality costs,
although the relationship should perhaps not be interpreted as
causal. Still, taken together, this suggests that consolidation can
improve banking markets.

Regulation is helping, but can get in the way
The Banking Union has made deals easier to assess: the ECB has
become the single prudential supervisor for large banks, capital
rules are more consistent, while resolution planning is now much

clearer than it was even a decade ago. But as the Draghi report
noted, the incomplete Banking Union means Europe’s banks remain
less proﬁtable than their US counterparts.
There is a shift underway, though. The EU Commission plans to
table a broad Banking Package in the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, with
measures covering cross-border capital and liquidity management,
deposit insurance, prudential rules and other items. For M&A, the
most important proposals include those to give banking groups

more ﬂexibility to allocate capital and liquidity more eﬃciently
within cross-border groups, while also applying more consistent

### Page 8

treatment to intra-group exposures. These remain proposals rather
than agreed implementations, meaning their ﬁnal scope and
timetable remain uncertain.
The AFME estimates that regulatory ring-fencing rules mean that
some €225bn of capital and €250bn of liquidity is trapped within
subsidiaries, which banks cannot freely move between parent and
subsidiaries. Freeing this up could also support more cross-border
transactions.

capital and liquidity

Capital Liquidity
Source: ECB, ING
The European Commission is also bringing forward a new proposal
to replace the European Deposit Insurance Scheme (EDIS), which
could reduce national ring-fencing incentives and encourage more
cross-border M&A. CMDI reform has been passed this year, which
could make it easier to deal with failing banks, potentially making
consolidation easier as well, although the direct impact on M&A is

unclear.
The Danish compromise, which is now embedded into CRR3, allows
banks to risk-weight certain insurance subsidiaries rather than
deduct them from regulatory capital. This was clariﬁed by the EBA
in January 2026, and some banks such as UniCredit have received
authorisation to use the Danish Compromise methodology for its
insurance subsidiaries. This has improved the economics of

### Page 9

bancassurance and could support further bank-insurance
transactions.
On the other hand, national regulatory and political intervention
has also put the brakes on M&A in some cases, raising execution
costs and uncertainty. BBVA’s proposed acquisition of Sabadell in
2024 illustrates the problem. It took 539 days from the
announcement in May 2024 to the oﬀer failing in October 2025.
For us, the message seems clear that European regulators are
seeking to improve bank competitiveness, and they see

consolidation as an important part of this. Looking ahead,
regulation can improve the plumbing, but it is not a dealmaker.
Why 2027 should stay busy
The starting point for 2027 remains supportive, with bank
proﬁtability continuing to hold up well in 2026 while capital levels
remain solid. While the geopolitical, inﬂation and interest rate
backdrop poses headwinds, we still expect acquisition activity to
remain high. On the other hand, management teams will

increasingly need to choose between retaining greater capital
headroom in a less market-friendly environment, shareholder
payouts, or M&A.
The most likely deals are still domestic. Italy remains the M&A
market of choice, but other geographies could also stand out. The
Nordics are likely to see more partnerships and tech- and wealth-
focused deals. Spain and Portugal are already quite concentrated,
so we could see more bolt-ons. In the UK, challenger banks and

neobanks are gaining ground while deposit competition remains
intense, potentially leading to more activity. The Benelux has
potential catalysts following a series of government ownership
reductions and increasing inorganic ambitions.
Cross-border activity could also remain elevated, but we think the
real litmus test remains the UniCredit-Commerzbank deal, including
what happens after completion in terms of execution and
integration, a process that is likely to take a few years. The

regulatory environment should also become increasingly more

supportive for cross-border M&A from 2027 onwards.
Overall, 2027 could be another interesting year.
M&A is here to stay
European bank M&A has picked up in recent years, and we expect
more activity again in 2027, although we do not see a big bang. For
investors, positioning is inherently speculative. For bondholders, the
main way to gain is by owning the target entity's bonds. However,
this is not always the case, and bigger doesn’t always mean better,

as credit ratings don’t necessarily improve either following an
acquisition or merger.
We are not suggesting that a major change in the European
banking landscape is imminent, as many obstacles remain,
particularly with cross-border deals. But the direction of travel is
interesting: larger banks often outperform smaller banks, while
regulators continue to see beneﬁts from a deeper, more integrated
European banking union. Still, European banking markets remain

fragmented to diﬀering degrees, both between countries and
compared to the US, presenting both opportunities and obstacles.

THE WEEKLY AGGREGATES REPORT
The Weekly Aggregates report provides updates on aggregate earnings revisions, growth rates and valuations. The 
numbers in the "Global Datapoints", "U.S. Datapoints" and "Ancillary Data" sections are updated weekly.
This report combines I/B/E/S's unrivaled historical earnings database, in depth coverage of Wall Street analysts' 
bottom-up corporate earnings estimates, and the analytic capabilities of the Proprietary Research group 
and desk-top solutions.

ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_875
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing
declines in petrol and diesel prices.
• Under an escalation scenario,
renewed conflict and deeper supply
disruptions could push Brent towards
US$150/bbl in H1 2027, prolonging
elevated fuel and LNG prices.

• Under an early resolution scenario, a
durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards
US$65/bbl by Q4 2027 and bringing
fuel and LNG prices down faster.

Brent crude
